  167. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-167 "Jump up")** [Ellis & Van Elst 1999](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFEllisVan_Elst1999); a closer look at the singularity itself is taken in [Börner 1993](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFB%C3%B6rner1993), sec. 1.2
  168. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-168 "Jump up")** Here one should remind to the well-known fact that the important "quasi-optical" singularities of the so-called [eikonal approximations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eikonal_approximation "Eikonal approximation") of many wave equations, namely the "[caustics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caustic_\(mathematics\) "Caustic \(mathematics\)")", are resolved into finite peaks beyond that approximation.
  169. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-169 "Jump up")** Namely when there are [trapped null surfaces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trapped_null_surface "Trapped null surface"), cf. [Penrose 1965](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFPenrose1965)
  170. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-170 "Jump up")** [Hawking 1966](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFHawking1966)
  171. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-171 "Jump up")** The conjecture was made in [Belinskii, Khalatnikov & Lifschitz 1971](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFBelinskiiKhalatnikovLifschitz1971); for a more recent review, see [Berger 2002](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFBerger2002). An accessible exposition is given by [Garfinkle 2007](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFGarfinkle2007)
  172. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-172 "Jump up")** The restriction to future singularities naturally excludes initial singularities such as the big bang singularity, which in principle be visible to observers at later cosmic time. The cosmic censorship conjecture was first presented in [Penrose 1969](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFPenrose1969); a textbook-level account is given in [Wald 1984](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFWald1984), pp. 302–305. For numerical results, see the review [Berger 2002](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFBerger2002), sec. 2.1
  173. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-173 "Jump up")** [Hawking & Ellis 1973](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFHawkingEllis1973), sec. 7.1
  174. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-174 "Jump up")** [Arnowitt, Deser & Misner 1962](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFArnowittDeserMisner1962); for a pedagogical introduction, see [Misner, Thorne & Wheeler 1973](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFMisnerThorneWheeler1973), § 21.4–§ 21.7
  175. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-175 "Jump up")** [Fourès-Bruhat 1952](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFFour%C3%A8s-Bruhat1952) and [Bruhat 1962](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFBruhat1962); for a pedagogical introduction, see [Wald 1984](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFWald1984), ch. 10; an online review can be found in [Reula 1998](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFReula1998)
  176. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-176 "Jump up")** [Gourgoulhon 2007](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFGourgoulhon2007); for a review of the basics of numerical relativity, including the problems arising from the peculiarities of Einstein's equations, see [Lehner 2001](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFLehner2001)
  177. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-177 "Jump up")** [Misner, Thorne & Wheeler 1973](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFMisnerThorneWheeler1973), § 20.4
  178. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-178 "Jump up")** [Arnowitt, Deser & Misner 1962](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFArnowittDeserMisner1962)
  179. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-179 "Jump up")** [Komar 1959](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFKomar1959); for a pedagogical introduction, see [Wald 1984](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFWald1984), sec. 11.2; although defined in a totally different way, it can be shown to be equivalent to the ADM mass for stationary spacetimes, cf. [Ashtekar & Magnon-Ashtekar 1979](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFAshtekarMagnon-Ashtekar1979)
  180. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-180 "Jump up")** For a pedagogical introduction, see [Wald 1984](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFWald1984), sec. 11.2
  181. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-181 "Jump up")** [Wald 1984](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFWald1984), p. 295 and refs therein; this is important for questions of stability—if there were [negative mass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Negative_mass "Negative mass") states, then flat, empty Minkowski space, which has mass zero, could evolve into these states
  182. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-182 "Jump up")** [Townsend 1997](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFTownsend1997), ch. 5
  183. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-183 "Jump up")** Such quasi-local mass–energy definitions are the [Hawking energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawking_energy "Hawking energy"), [Geroch energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geroch_energy "Geroch energy"), or Penrose's quasi-local energy–momentum based on [twistor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twistor_theory "Twistor theory") methods; cf. the review article [Szabados 2004](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFSzabados2004)
  184. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-184 "Jump up")** An overview of quantum theory can be found in standard textbooks such as [Messiah 1999](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFMessiah1999); a more elementary account is given in [Hey & Walters 2003](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFHeyWalters2003)
  185. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-185 "Jump up")** [Ramond 1990](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFRamond1990), [Weinberg 1995](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFWeinberg1995), [Peskin & Schroeder 1995](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFPeskinSchroeder1995); a more accessible overview is [Auyang 1995](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFAuyang1995)
  186. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-186 "Jump up")** [Wald 1994](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFWald1994), [Birrell & Davies 1984](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFBirrellDavies1984)
  187. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-187 "Jump up")** For Hawking radiation [Hawking 1975](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFHawking1975), [Wald 1975](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFWald1975); an accessible introduction to black hole evaporation can be found in [Traschen 2000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFTraschen2000)
  188. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-188 "Jump up")** [Wald 2001](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFWald2001), ch. 3
  189. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-189 "Jump up")** Put simply, matter is the source of spacetime curvature, and once matter has quantum properties, we can expect spacetime to have them as well. Cf. [Carlip 2001](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFCarlip2001), sec. 2
  190. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-190 "Jump up")** [Schutz 2003](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFSchutz2003), p. 407
  191. ^ [Jump up to: _**a**_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-Hamber_2009_191-0) [_**b**_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-Hamber_2009_191-1) [Hamber 2009](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFHamber2009)
  192. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-192 "Jump up")** A timeline and overview can be found in [Rovelli 2000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFRovelli2000)
  193. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-193 "Jump up")** ['t Hooft & Veltman 1974](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREF't_HooftVeltman1974)
  194. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-194 "Jump up")** [Donoghue 1995](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFDonoghue1995)
  195. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-195 "Jump up")** In particular, a perturbative technique known as [renormalization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renormalization "Renormalization"), an integral part of deriving predictions which take into account higher-energy contributions, cf. [Weinberg 1996](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFWeinberg1996), ch. 17, 18, fails in this case; cf. [Veltman 1975](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFVeltman1975), [Goroff & Sagnotti 1985](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFGoroffSagnotti1985); for a recent comprehensive review of the failure of perturbative renormalizability for quantum gravity see [Hamber 2009](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFHamber2009)
  196. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-196 "Jump up")** An accessible introduction at the undergraduate level can be found in [Zwiebach 2004](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFZwiebach2004); more complete overviews can be found in [Polchinski 1998a](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFPolchinski1998a) and [Polchinski 1998b](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFPolchinski1998b)
  197. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-197 "Jump up")** At the energies reached in current experiments, these strings are indistinguishable from point-like particles, but, crucially, different [modes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Normal_mode "Normal mode") of oscillation of one and the same type of fundamental string appear as particles with different ([electric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_charge "Electric charge") and other) [charges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charge_\(physics\) "Charge \(physics\)"), e.g. [Ibanez 2000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFIbanez2000). The theory is successful in that one mode will always correspond to a [graviton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graviton "Graviton"), the [messenger particle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Messenger_particle "Messenger particle") of gravity, e.g. [Green, Schwarz & Witten 1987](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFGreenSchwarzWitten1987), sec. 2.3, 5.3
  198. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-198 "Jump up")** [Green, Schwarz & Witten 1987](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFGreenSchwarzWitten1987), sec. 4.2
  199. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-199 "Jump up")** [Weinberg 2000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFWeinberg2000), ch. 31
  200. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-200 "Jump up")** [Townsend 1996](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFTownsend1996), [Duff 1996](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFDuff1996)
  201. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-201 "Jump up")** [Kuchař 1973](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFKucha%C5%991973), sec. 3
  202. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-202 "Jump up")** These variables represent geometric gravity using mathematical analogues of [electric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_field "Electric field") and [magnetic fields](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magnetic_field "Magnetic field"); cf. [Ashtekar 1986](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFAshtekar1986), [Ashtekar 1987](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFAshtekar1987)

  213. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-213 "Jump up")** A review of the various problems and the techniques being developed to overcome them, see [Lehner 2002](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFLehner2002)
  214. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-214 "Jump up")** See [Bartusiak 2000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFBartusiak2000) for an account up to that year; up-to-date news can be found on the websites of major detector collaborations such as [GEO600](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GEO600 "GEO600") and [LIGO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LIGO "LIGO")
  215. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#cite_ref-215 "Jump up")** For the most recent papers on gravitational wave polarizations of inspiralling compact binaries, see [Blanchet et al. 2008](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFBlanchetFayeIyerSinha2008), and [Arun et al. 2008](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFArunBlanchetIyerQusailah2008); for a review of work on compact binaries, see [Blanchet 2006](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFBlanchet2006) and [Futamase & Itoh 2006](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFFutamaseItoh2006); for a general review of experimental tests of general relativity, see [Will 2006](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity#CITEREFWill2006).
